1     The compact-simple-group theorem and proof boundary
1.1    Problem and result
The constructive Yang–Mills problem asks for a nontrivial quantum gauge field theory on R4 satisfying
the Osterwalder–Schrader and Wightman requirements and possessing a strictly positive mass gap [1].
The physical scale, thermodynamic limit, zero lattice spacing, Euclidean covariance, local spectral content,
and non-abelian field geometry must be controlled in one compatible construction.
   Fix throughout a compact connected simple Lie group G, a faithful unitary representation ρ : G → U (Vρ )
of dimension dρ , and a positive normalization of the invariant quadratic form for which the adjoint color
factor is denoted CA (G). The Wilson plaquette function is
                                                        1
                                                                    
                                   wG,ρ (U ) = cρ 1 −     Re Tr ρ(U ) ,
                                                       dρ
where cρ > 0 is chosen once so that the Hessian at the identity is the quadratic form used by the
background-field coupling coordinate u = g 2 . This choice is a finite linear normalization of the bare
Wilson coupling and is part of the fixed-group input register.
Theorem 1.1 (Four-dimensional Yang–Mills construction for fixed compact simple G). For every fixed
pair (G, ρ) as above, there is a directed sequence of reflection-positive Wilson lattice measures, with
microscopic spacing tending to zero and periodic volume tending to infinity, whose local gauge-invariant
curvature-jet Schwinger functions converge to a nontrivial Euclidean field theory on R4 .
   The limiting Schwinger family is tempered, permutation symmetric, reflection positive, translation
continuous, exponentially clustering, and O(4) covariant. Osterwalder–Schrader reconstruction yields a
positive-energy Poincaré-covariant local theory with a unique vacuum and a number m⋆ (G) > 0 such that
                                              H Ω⊥ ≥ m⋆ (G).
On the centered invariant weak trajectory, the exact scale-two coupling map is
                                            11 CA (G) log 2
                           u(2L) = u(L) +                   u(L)2 + OG (u(L)3 ),
                                                 24π 2
with uniform volume and cutoff control in the permanent weak regime. The reconstructed theory contains
a nonzero reflected local curvature vector and positive spectral mass in a finite interval [m⋆ (G), M⋆ (G)].
1.2    Fixed-group conventions and finite register
Write g = Lie(G) and dG = dim G. All Haar measures are normalized. The finite-volume approximants
use the ordinary periodic link space GE(Λ) with periodic gauge transformations; no twisted boundary
condition or nontrivial finite-volume bundle sector is summed. This is sufficient for the claimed local
theory on the contractible space R4 . The bi-invariant metric, exponential coordinates, Laplacian ∆G ,
Casimir eigenvalues, and operator norms are those determined by the declared quadratic form. In
curvature formulas, Tr(XY ) denotes this normalized invariant bilinear pairing; raw representation traces
are written Tr ρ(·). Let                     
                                              dG                    dG
                                      sG :=       + 1,      2sG >       .
                                               4                      2
The fixed-group constant register CG consists of the finite quantities used below: the injectivity and
convexity radii, a positive local Karcher radius, finite holomorphic BCH and logarithm bounds through
order 2sG + 3, the faithful-action Hessian and complement charge, the Peter–Weyl constant, the finite
ordinary-periodic toron/seam template register, the adjoint color factor, and the finite invariant-jet
dimensions through the retained degree. Every statement called uniform is uniform in cutoff, volume,
directed depth, periodic wrapping, admitted exterior histories, and the finite reflected template family,
but its constants may depend on CG .
Proposition 1.2 (Reflection-positive Wilson input). For every finite periodic lattice Λ and u > 0, in the
ordinary periodic link sector,
                                                "                    #

is gauge invariant and reflection positive for coordinate reflections. Moreover wG,ρ ≥ 0, its zero set is the
identity, and on one fixed identity neighborhood
                                     cG ∥X∥2 ≤ wG,ρ (eX ) ≤ CG ∥X∥2 .


                                                     3
Proof. The normalized character satisfies Re Tr ρ(U ) ≤ dρ , with equality only when ρ(U ) = I; faithfulness
gives U = e. The Hessian statement follows from the chosen normalization and compactness. Up to a field-
independent constant, each plaquette factor is exp{t(χρ +χρ )} with t > 0. Its power series is a nonnegative
linear combination of characters of tensor products of ρ and ρ̄, and their irreducible decompositions
have nonnegative integer multiplicities. The standard character-expansion reflection-positivity argument
therefore applies.

1.3     Proof architecture
The proof has four stages. First, the exact Karcher block is placed in a complete full-alias normal chart
and combined with a charged chart-complement partition, permanent analytic domain, marked connected
logarithm, Wick-stable jet block, and centered invariant graph. The folded spacetime symbol tensors
with Ig , while all nonlinear estimates are admitted by the finite register CG .
   Second, exact background-scheme conjugacy identifies the universal marginal coefficient      and a directed
schedule reaches the compact matching scale. The higher product Sobolev operator p (1 − ∆p )sG replaces
                                                                                       Q
the rank-one order and supplies the fixed-G Fourier–Wiener rows.
   Third, complete Haar and optical variables are kept in one partition function. A mixed replica–
BKAR forest makes every root-to-root path pay separated Haar contraction, optical covariance decay,
or pre-existing support charge. Ordinary-periodic toron backgrounds, seams, reflections, and wrapping
certificates enter only through the finite template theorem of Appendix D.
   Finally, a least-label source forest transports the complete invariant-jet system. The degree-four block
is group-independent because a compact simple Lie algebra has a unique invariant symmetric bilinear
form; possible cubic color tensors first enter the finite dimension-six block. Ward identities, rotation-defect
estimates, reflection-positive recombination, and Osterwalder–Schrader reconstruction give covariance,
the unique vacuum, the gap, and noncollapse.

Only the theorem-level inputs in Table 1 are imported. The fixed-group geometry, action charge, higher
Sobolev landing, periodic-toron template theorem, invariant-jet admission, matching constants, and
noncollapse source are internal.

1.5     Self-contained relation to the rank-one construction
The companion SU(2) manuscript [14] is a completely expanded rank-one specialization and a useful
independent check. It is not a logical input to Theorem 1.1: every common analytic theorem used here is
stated and proved in this paper, and every new fixed-group admission is printed below. Appendix F gives
a theorem-level specialization map so that a referee can identify which arguments are color-free, which
tensor with Ig , and which require new group data.
1.6     Scope
The theorem concerns the local gauge-invariant curvature-jet observable sector for each fixed compact
connected simple G. It does not assert a bound uniform over groups, a simultaneous large-rank limit,
control of every extended Wilson-line or generalized-symmetry sector, or uniqueness of the continuum
limit outside the directed construction. Quotient groups are included through their actual compact link
group. The construction uses only the ordinary periodic finite-volume sector and retains all flat toron
backgrounds occurring in that sector; no claim about a sum over twisted boundary conditions is made or
needed.

2     Fixed-group Wilson geometry and Karcher blocking
Let Λ be a finite periodic four-dimensional lattice and use the reflection-positive Wilson measure of
Proposition 1.2. For each oriented retained link, use the straight two-link path with weight 7/8 and the
forty-eight signed-corner staples with weight 1/384 each. On a fixed strongly convex identity tube, define
the retained link as their weighted Karcher mean in the bi-invariant metric.
Lemma 2.1 (Fixed-group convex Karcher and holomorphic atlas). There are radii 0 < r0,G < r1,G
such that the squared-distance functions, product, inverse, exponential, logarithm, and weighted Karcher
critical point extend holomorphically to the corresponding local complexification tube. The Karcher Hessian
is bounded below there by a positive constant depending only on G. All derivatives through arbitrary
prescribed finite order are bounded on the smaller tube.
Proof. A compact Lie group with a bi-invariant analytic metric has positive injectivity and strong-convexity
radii. The sectional curvature is bounded because G is compact, and the Hessian comparison theorem
gives a positive lower Hessian bound on a sufficiently small ball. In a faithful matrix representation
the local complexification is an open neighborhood in the complexified group; multiplication, inversio

form; possible cubic color tensors first enter the finite dimension-six block. Ward identities, rotation-defect
estimates, reflection-positive recombination, and Osterwalder–Schrader reconstruction give covariance,
the unique vacuum, the gap, and noncollapse.

2     Fixed-group Wilson geometry and Karcher blocking
Let Λ be a finite periodic four-dimensional lattice and use the reflection-positive Wilson measure of
Proposition 1.2. For each oriented retained link, use the straight two-link path with weight 7/8 and the
forty-eight signed-corner staples with weight 1/384 each. On a fixed strongly convex identity tube, define
the retained link as their weighted Karcher mean in the bi-invariant metric.
Lemma 2.1 (Fixed-group convex Karcher and holomorphic atlas). There are radii 0 < r0,G < r1,G
such that the squared-distance functions, product, inverse, exponential, logarithm, and weighted Karcher
critical point extend holomorphically to the corresponding local complexification tube. The Karcher Hessian
is bounded below there by a positive constant depending only on G. All derivatives through arbitrary
prescribed finite order are bounded on the smaller tube.
Proof. A compact Lie group with a bi-invariant analytic metric has positive injectivity and strong-convexity
radii. The sectional curvature is bounded because G is compact, and the Hessian comparison theorem
gives a positive lower Hessian bound on a sufficiently small ball. In a faithful matrix representation
the local complexification is an open neighborhood in the complexified group; multiplication, inversion,
exponential, and the principal logarithm are holomorphic there. The analytic implicit-function theorem
applied to the Karcher critical equation gives the holomorphic mean. Cauchy estimates on nested compact
tubes give every fixed derivative order; standard compact Lie-group analytic facts are summarized in
[15].
Proposition 2.2 (Exact block covariance and local submersion). The retained map is gauge covariant,
orientation covariant, signed-hypercubic covariant, and aligned-reflection covariant. Its linearization at
the flat connection has full retained rank. On one fixed weak tube it admits a finite-volume analytic
normal section whose matrix kernel and first three marked derivatives are exponentially local, uniformly
in volume.
Proof. Every path has the same endpoints, and the weighted squared-distance functional is invariant
under simultaneous left and right multiplication. Uniqueness of the Karcher minimizer gives gauge and
orientation covariance. The signed-corner path family is invariant under the lattice symmetry group.
At the flat point, the exact Fourier symbol is the color-free spacetime symbol tensored with Ig and has
acoustic value 2I ⊗ Ig and a uniformly gapped normal frame. The full-alias section and normal-fiber
contraction are proved in the later normal-chart chapters.
Proposition 2.3 (Exact coarea–fiber identity). At finite volume, in the normal fiber chart,

                                    dU = J0 det(I + DN P )−1 dW dζ,


                                                     5
where the determinant is the ordinary finite-dimensional determinant. The fixed linear determinant is
owned once as a scalar normalization, and the field-dependent logarithm is one connected analytic activity.

1.5     Self-contained relation to the rank-one construction
The companion SU(2) manuscript [14] is a completely expanded rank-one specialization and a useful
independent check. It is not a logical input to Theorem 1.1: every common analytic theorem used here is
stated and proved in this paper, and every new fixed-group admission is printed below. Appendix F gives
a theorem-level specialization map so that a referee can identify which arguments are color-free, which
tensor with Ig , and which require new group data.
1.6     Scope
The theorem concerns the local gauge-invariant curvature-jet observable sector for each fixed compact
connected simple G. It does not assert a bound uniform over groups, a simultaneous large-rank limit,
control of every extended Wilson-line or generalized-symmetry sector, or uniqueness of the continuum
limit outside the directed construction. Quotient groups are included through their actual compact link
group. The construction uses only the ordinary periodic finite-volume sector and retains all flat toron
backgrounds occurring in that sector; no claim about a sum over twisted boundary conditions is made or
needed.

2     Fixed-group Wilson geometry and Karcher blocking
Let Λ be a finite periodic four-dimensional lattice and use the reflection-positive Wilson measure of
Proposition 1.2. For each oriented retained link, use the straight two-link path with weight 7/8 and the
forty-eight signed-corner staples with weight 1/384 each. On a fixed strongly convex identity tube, define
the retained link as their weighted Karcher mean in the bi-invariant metric.
Lemma 2.1 (Fixed-group convex Karcher and holomorphic atlas). There are radii 0 < r0,G < r1,G
such that the squared-distance functions, product, inverse, exponential, logarithm, and weighted Karcher
critical point extend holomorphically to the corresponding local complexification tube. The Karcher Hessian
is bounded below there by a positive constant depending only on G. All derivatives through arbitrary
prescribed finite order are bounded on the smaller tube.
Proof. A compact Lie group with a bi-invariant analytic metric has positive injectivity and strong-convexity
radii. The sectional curvature is bounded because G is compact, and the Hessian comparison theorem
gives a positive lower Hessian bound on a sufficiently small ball. In a faithful matrix representation
the local complexification is an open neighborhood in the complexified group; multiplication, inversion,
exponential, and the principal logarithm are holomorphic there. The analytic implicit-function theorem
applied to the Karcher critical equation gives the holomorphic mean. Cauchy estimates on nested compact
tubes give every fixed derivative order; standard compact Lie-group analytic facts are summarized in
[15].
Proposition 2.2 (Exact block covariance and local submersion). The retained map is gauge covariant,
orientation covariant, signed-hypercubic covariant, and aligned-reflection covariant. Its linearization at
the flat connection has full retained rank. On one fixed weak tube it admits a finite-volume analytic
normal section whose matrix kernel and first three marked derivatives are exponentially local, uniformly
in volume.
Proof. Every path has the same endpoints, and the weighted squared-distance functional is invariant
under simultaneous left and right multiplication. Uniqueness of the Karcher minimizer gives gauge and
orientation covariance. The signed-corner path family is invariant under the lattice symmetry group.
At the flat point, the exact Fourier symbol is the color-free spacetime symbol tensored with Ig and has
acoustic value 2I ⊗ Ig and a uniformly gapped normal frame. The full-alias section and normal-fiber
contraction are proved in the later normal-chart chapters.
Proposition 2.3 (Exact coarea–fiber identity). At finite volume, in the normal fiber chart,

                                    dU = J0 det(I + DN P )−1 dW dζ,


                                                     5
where the determinant is the ordinary finite-dimensional determinant. The fixed linear determinant is
owned once as a scalar normalization, and the field-dependent logarithm is one connected analytic activity.

1.5     Self-contained relation to the rank-one construction
The companion SU(2) manuscript [14] is a completely expanded rank-one specialization and a useful
independent check. It is not a logical input to Theorem 1.1: every common analytic theorem used here is
stated and proved in this paper, and every new fixed-group admission is printed below. Appendix F gives
a theorem-level specialization map so that a referee can identify which arguments are color-free, which
tensor with Ig , and which require new group data.
1.6     Scope
The theorem concerns the local gauge-invariant curvature-jet observable sector for each fixed compact
connected simple G. It does not assert a bound uniform over groups, a simultaneous large-rank limit,
control of every extended Wilson-line or generalized-symmetry sector, or uniqueness of the continuum
limit outside the directed construction. Quotient groups are included through their actual compact link
group. The construction uses only the ordinary periodic finite-volume sector and retains all flat toron
backgrounds occurring in that sector; no claim about a sum over twisted boundary conditions is made or
needed.

2     Fixed-group Wilson geometry and Karcher blocking
Let Λ be a finite periodic four-dimensional lattice and use the reflection-positive Wilson measure of
Proposition 1.2. For each oriented retained link, use the straight two-link path with weight 7/8 and the
forty-eight signed-corner staples with weight 1/384 each. On a fixed strongly convex identity tube, define
the retained link as their weighted Karcher mean in the bi-invariant metric.
Lemma 2.1 (Fixed-group convex Karcher and holomorphic atlas). There are radii 0 < r0,G < r1,G
such that the squared-distance functions, product, inverse, exponential, logarithm, and weighted Karcher
critical point extend holomorphically to the corresponding local complexification tube. The Karcher Hessian
is bounded below there by a positive constant depending only on G. All derivatives through arbitrary
prescribed finite order are bounded on the smaller tube.
Proof. A compact Lie group with a bi-invariant analytic metric has positive injectivity and strong-convexity
radii. The sectional curvature is bounded because G is compact, and the Hessian comparison theorem
gives a positive lower Hessian bound on a sufficiently small ball. In a faithful matrix representation
the local complexification is an open neighborhood in the complexified group; multiplication, inversion,
exponential, and the principal logarithm are holomorphic there. The analytic implicit-function theorem
applied to the Karcher critical equation gives the holomorphic mean. Cauchy estimates on nested compact
tubes give every fixed derivative order; standard compact Lie-group analytic facts are summarized in
[15].
Proposition 2.2 (Exact block covariance and local submersion). The retained map is gauge covariant,
orientation covariant, signed-hypercubic covariant, and aligned-reflection covariant. Its linearization at
the flat connection has full retained rank. On one fixed weak tube it admits a finite-volume analytic
normal section whose matrix kernel and first three marked derivatives are exponentially local, uniformly
in volume.
Proof. Every path has the same endpoints, and the weighted squared-distance functional is invariant
under simultaneous left and right multiplication. Uniqueness of the Karcher minimizer gives gauge and
orientation covariance. The signed-corner path family is invariant under the lattice symmetry group.
At the flat point, the exact Fourier symbol is the color-free spacetime symbol tensored with Ig and has
acoustic value 2I ⊗ Ig and a uniformly gapped normal frame. The full-alias section and normal-fiber
contraction are proved in the later normal-chart chapters.
Proposition 2.3 (Exact coarea–fiber identity). At finite volume, in the normal fiber chart,

                                    dU = J0 det(I + DN P )−1 dW dζ,


                                                     5
where the determinant is the ordinary finite-dimensional determinant. The fixed linear determinant is
owned once as a scalar normalization, and the field-dependent logarithm is one connected analytic activity.

2.2    Exact normal fiber chart and coarea
Let Xm , Ym be exponentially weighted Banach coordinate spaces and let

                    L : Xm → Ym ,         P : Ym → Xm ,              LP = I,        ∥P ∥m = p < 1.

Put K = ker L and write

                            B(x) = Lx + N (x),              N (0) = 0,       DN (0) = 0.


                                                            7
Theorem 2.5 (Normal fiber chart). For w ∈ Ym and ζ ∈ K, consider
                                       F (y; w, ζ) = y + N (P y + ζ) − w.
Assume on a product ball that
                                       η := sup ∥DN (P y + ζ)P ∥m < 1.
Then there is a unique analytic solution y = T (w, ζ) and
                                            PN (w, ζ) := P T (w, ζ) + ζ
is an exact fiber chart:
                                                  B(PN (w, ζ)) = w.
If A = I + DN (PN )P , then
                                Dw T = A− 1,            Dζ T = −A− 1DN (PN )|K ,
and hence
                           Dw PN = P A− 1,           Dζ PN = IK − P A− 1DN (PN )|K .
All derivatives through order three are finite sums of products of A− 1, P, D2 N, D3 N and are exponentially
local in the same Banach matrix algebra.
Proof. For fixed (w, ζ), the map
                                                 y 7→ w − N (P y + ζ)
is an η-contraction on a sufficiently chosen invariant ball. Banach’s fixed-point theorem gives T . The
exact fiber identity follows by substitution. Differentiating
                                              T + N (P T + ζ) = w
gives the displayed first derivatives. Repeated differentiation gives the second- and third-order Bell-
polynomial formulas. Since ∥A− 1∥ ≤ (1 − η)− 1, every term is bounded in the same exponential matrix
algebra.

is an η-contraction on a sufficiently chosen invariant ball. Banach’s fixed-point theorem gives T . The
exact fiber identity follows by substitution. Differentiating
                                              T + N (P T + ζ) = w
gives the displayed first derivatives. Repeated differentiation gives the second- and third-order Bell-
polynomial formulas. Since ∥A− 1∥ ≤ (1 − η)− 1, every term is bounded in the same exponential matrix
algebra.

2.3     Nonlinear forty-nine-path section
Lemma 2.8 (Finite-cell analytic atlas and uniform Cauchy bounds). Let P49 be the finite path atlas
for one retained cell and its finite aligned-reflection template family. There exist fixed radii 0 < η0 < η1 ,
uniform under the standing convention, such that the product exponential, multiplication, inversion,
logarithm, and Karcher-mean maps defining every template extend holomorphically from Tη0 to Tη1 .
   Write the canonically rescaled local block as

                             B(x) = Lx + N (x),       N (0) = 0,             DN (0) = 0.

For every exponential kernel rate 0 < m < mP and r = 2, 3, there is a finite constant

                                             CN,r (m; η0 , η1 ) < ∞

such that
                                    sup ∥Dr N (x)∥Km ≤ CN,r (m; η0 , η1 ),
                                   x∈Tη0

uniformly under the standing convention. The same constants, enlarged by a finite maximum, serve the
aligned-reflection templates.
Proof. For one retained cell the block depends on finitely many g coordinates. The exponential and
multiplication maps are entire, while inversion and the principal logarithm are holomorphic on a fixed
neighborhood of the identity. At the flat field the Karcher critical point has positive Hessian on the
retained tangent space; the analytic implicit-function theorem therefore supplies a unique holomorphic
Karcher mean on a fixed complex neighborhood. The path family and the reflected template family are
finite, so one may choose common nested radii 0 < η0 < η1 for all of them. On the larger finite-dimensional
complex polydisc, every elementary map and their finite compositions are holomorphic and uniformly
bounded. Cauchy’s integral estimate on the smaller polydisc gives uniform derivative bounds through
order three with denominator powers of η1 − η0 .
   The global block is obtained by translating this fixed finite cell. Each output row meets only a fixed
number of input cells, and each input column enters only a fixed number of output cells. Multiplying the
finite-cell Cauchy bounds by these fixed incidence numbers gives the two-sided exponentially weighted
Schur-kernel bounds. These incidence numbers do not depend on the total volume or preceding weak
depth. The aligned-reflection family is finite, so one takes a finite maximum.

has a unique solution y = T (w, 0) in ∥y∥Km ≤ 2ρm . The zero-detail normal section

                                              S(w) := P0 T (w, 0)

satisfies
                                  B(S(

All derivatives through order three are finite sums of products of A− 1, P, D2 N, D3 N and are exponentially
local in the same Banach matrix algebra.

                                               y + N (P0 y) = w

                                              S(w) := P0 T (w, 0)

                                  B(S(w)) = w,        ∥S(w)∥Km ≤ 2pm ρm .

Proof. For fixed w, define
                                            Φw (y) := w − N (P0 y).

Put g = u and Rg = g −2ε with 0 < ε < 1/6. The symbol χ > 0 denotes the curvature-bad threshold
in this section and in the following two irregular-sector sections; the rooted support exponent remains
denoted by κ elsewhere.
   For an enlarged finite tube cell Q+ define the faithful fine Wilson charge and retained coherent deficit
by the same representation-normalized plaquette function used in the microscopic measure:
                                     X                              X
                        AQ+ (U ) =      wG,ρ (Up ),     DQ (U ) =       wG,ρ (Wp′ ).
                                    p⊂Q+                            p′ ⊂Q′

Let TQ be a fixed smooth invariant dispersion function formed from the squared relative-loop distances of
the forty-nine path inputs. It vanishes on the flat set, its small sublevel set lies inside the common Karcher
chart, and it uses only one fixed enlarged-cell template. On that sublevel set the retained holonomies
Wp′ are defined by the local Karcher atlas. Multiply the displayed retained deficit by a fixed chart cutoff
that is one on a prescribed smaller Karcher sublevel and zero before the edge of the larger Karcher tube;
this gives a smooth invariant extension to the complete finite-cell configuration space. The threshold tch
below is chosen so that the entire curvature-label support lies where this cutoff equals one.
Lemma 3.1 (Representation-normalized charge and derivative ledger). For every fixed G, faithful ρ,
and integer 0 ≤ r ≤ 2sG + 3, there are finite constants depending only on the fixed cell atlas such that

                                wG,ρ (U ) ≥ 0,      wG,ρ (U ) = 0 ⇐⇒ U = e,

                             cG ∥X∥2 ≤ wG,ρ (eX ) ≤ CG ∥X∥2       (∥X∥ ≤ r0,G ),
and
             ∥DwG,ρ (U )∥ ≤ CG wG,ρ (U )1/2 ,       ∥Dr wG,ρ (U )∥ ≤ CG,r     (2 ≤ r ≤ 2sG + 3).
The same bounds, with finite template constants, hold after composition with every Wilson word, retained
plaquette word, reflected word, or seam word used below.
Proof. Nonnegativity and the zero set are Proposition 1.2. In the chosen invariant metric the Hessian of
wG,ρ at the identity is positive definite; Taylor’s theorem gives the two-sided quadratic comparison. The
first-derivative estimate follows from that comparison on the identity tube. On its compact complement
wG,ρ has a positive minimum while its derivative is bounded, so the same inequality holds globally after
enlarging the constant. All higher derivatives are bounded because wG,ρ is a smooth matrix coefficient
on compact G. Every word in the fixed path atlas is a finite composition of multiplication and inversion,
so the chain rule gives the asserted finite derivative bounds.

2.2    Exact normal fiber chart and coarea
Let Xm , Ym be exponentially weighted Banach coordinate spaces and let

                    L : Xm → Ym ,         P : Ym → Xm ,              LP = I,        ∥P ∥m = p < 1.

Put K = ker L and write

                            B(x) = Lx + N (x),              N (0) = 0,       DN (0) = 0.


                                                            7
Theorem 2.5 (Normal fiber chart). For w ∈ Ym and ζ ∈ K, consider
                                       F (y; w, ζ) = y + N (P y + ζ) − w.
Assume on a product ball that
                                       η := sup ∥DN (P y + ζ)P ∥m < 1.
Then there is a unique analytic solution y = T (w, ζ) and
                                            PN (w, ζ) := P T (w, ζ) + ζ
is an exact fiber chart:
                                                  B(PN (w, ζ)) = w.
If A = I + DN (PN )P , then
                                Dw T = A− 1,            Dζ T = −A− 1DN (PN )|K ,
and hence
                           Dw PN = P A− 1,           Dζ PN = IK − P A− 1DN (PN )|K .
All derivatives through order three are finite sums of products of A− 1, P, D2 N, D3 N and are exponentially
local in the same Banach matrix algebra.
Proof. For fixed (w, ζ), the map
                                                 y 7→ w − N (P y + ζ)
is an η-contraction on a sufficiently chosen invariant ball. Banach’s fixed-point theorem gives T . The
exact fiber identity follows by substitution. Differentiating
                                              T + N (P T + ζ) = w
gives the displayed first derivatives. Repeated differentiation gives the second- and third-order Bell-
polynomial formulas. Since ∥A− 1∥ ≤ (1 − η)− 1, every term is bounded in the same exponential matrix
algebra.

                                         ∥D2 S∥m ≤ 0.385828 M2 (ρ),
and
                               ∥D3 S∥m ≤ 0.280883 M3 (ρ) + 0.613449 M2 (ρ)2 .
Theorem 2.7 (Exact coarea factor). Let
                                                  J0 (y, ζ) = P y + ζ.
Relative to this fixed linear frame,
                                                                                     −1
                                 det DPN = det J0 det I + DN (PN )P                        .
Thus the field-dependent coarea logarithm is
                                          − log det I + DN (PN )P ,
                                                                 

while det J0 is a once-only field-independent normalization.
Proof. The chart factors as
                                       (w, ζ) 7→ (T (w, ζ), ζ) 7→ J0 (T, ζ).
The first derivative is block triangular:
                                                 A− 1   −A− 1DN |K
                                                                        
                                                                             .
                                                  0        IK
Its determinant is det A− 1.

2.3     Nonlinear forty-nine-path section
Lemma 2.8 (Finite-cell analytic atlas and uniform Cauchy bounds). Let P49 be the finite path atlas
for one retained cell and its finite aligned-reflection template family. There exist fixed radii 0 < η0 < η1 ,
uniform under the standing convention, such that the product exponential, multiplication, inversion,
logarithm, and Karcher-mean maps defining every template extend holomorphically from Tη0 to Tη1 .
   Write the canonically rescaled local block as

                             B(x) = Lx + N (x),       N (0) = 0,             DN (0) = 0.

For every exponential kernel rate 0 < m < mP and r = 2, 3, there is a finite constant

                                             CN,r (m; η0 , η1 ) < ∞

such that
                                    sup ∥Dr N (x)∥Km ≤ CN,r (m; η0 , η1 ),
                                   x∈Tη0

uniformly under the standing convention. The same constants, enlarged by a finite maximum, serve the
aligned-reflection templates.
Proof. For one retained cell the block depends on finitely many g coordinates. The exponential and
multiplication maps are entire, while inversion and the principal logarithm are holomorphic on a fixed
neighborhood of the identity. At the flat field the Karcher critical point has positive Hessian on the
retained tangent space; the analytic implicit-function theorem therefore supplies a unique holomorphic
Karcher mean on a fixed complex neighborhood. The path family and the reflected template family are
finite, so one may choose common nested radii 0 < η0 < η1 for all of them. On the larger finite-dimensional
complex polydisc, every elementary map and their finite compositions are holomorphic and uniformly
bounded. Cauchy’s integral estimate on the smaller polydisc gives uniform derivative bounds through
order three with denominator powers of η1 − η0 .
   The global block is obtained by translating this fixed finite cell. Each output row meets only a fixed
number of input cells, and each input column enters only a fixed number of output cells. Multiplying the
finite-cell Cauchy bounds by these fixed incidence numbers gives the two-sided exponentially weighted
Schur-kernel bounds. These incidence numbers do not depend on the total volume or preceding weak
depth. The aligned-reflection family is finite, so one takes a finite maximum.
Proposition 2.9 (Model-specific nonlinear normal section for the forty-nine-path block). Let

                             pm := ∥P0 ∥Km < 0.546,           C2 := CN,2 (m; η0 , η1 ),

and choose
                                                               1
                                                                    
                                                          η0
                                           ρm = min          ,           .
                                                          4 4pm C2
For every retained coordinate w with ∥w∥Km ≤ ρm , the retained-space equation

                                               y + N (P0 y) = w

has a unique solution y = T (w, 0) in ∥y∥Km ≤ 2ρm . The zero-detail normal section

                                              S(w) := P0 T (w, 0)

satisfies
                                  B(S(w)) = w,        ∥S(w)∥Km ≤ 2pm ρm .
It is analytic and exponentially quasi-local, and its first three marked derivatives have volume- and
depth-independent bounds. The same conclusion holds on the aligned-reflection templates after taking the
minimum radius over that finite family.
Proof. For fixed w, define
                                            Φw (y) := w − N (P0 y).
If ∥y∥ ≤ 2ρm , then ∥P0 y∥ ≤ η0 /2. Since DN (0) = 0, Lemma 2.8 gives
                                                                                 pm  1
                          ∥Dy Φw (y)∥ = ∥DN (P0 y)P0 ∥ ≤ 2p2m C2 ρm ≤               < .
                                                                                  2  2

                                                          9
The second-order integral formula gives
                                         1                            pm
                          ∥N (P0 y)∥ ≤     C2 ∥P0 y∥2 ≤ 2C2 p2m ρ2m ≤    ρm < ρm .
                                         2                             2
Thus Φw maps the 2ρm ball into itself and is a contraction. For its fixed point,

                             B(P0 T ) = LP0 T + N (P0 T ) = T + N (P0 T ) = w.

Analyticity follows from the analytic implicit-function theorem. Differentiation through order three gives
finite Bell polynomials in
                               (I + DN (S)P0 )−1 , P0 , D2 N, D3 N.
The inverse norm is below two and the other factors are bounded by Theorem 2.4 and Lemma 2.8.

3     Charged chart complement and irregular sector
          √
Put g = u and Rg = g −2ε with 0 < ε < 1/6. The symbol χ > 0 denotes the curvature-bad threshold
in this section and in the following two irregular-sector sections; the rooted support exponent remains
denoted by κ elsewhere.
   For an enlarged finite tube cell Q+ define the faithful fine Wilson charge and retained coherent deficit
by the same representation-normalized plaquette function used in the microscopic measure:
                                     X                              X
                        AQ+ (U ) =      wG,ρ (Up ),     DQ (U ) =       wG,ρ (Wp′ ).
                                    p⊂Q+                            p′ ⊂Q′

Let TQ be a fixed smooth invariant dispersion function formed from the squared relative-loop distances of
the forty-nine path inputs. It vanishes on the flat set, its small sublevel set lies inside the common Karcher
chart, and it uses only one fixed enlarged-cell template. On that sublevel set the retained holonomies
Wp′ are defined by the local Karcher atlas. Multiply the displayed retained deficit by a fixed chart cutoff
that is one on a prescribed smaller Karcher sublevel and zero before the edge of the larger Karcher tube;
this gives a smooth invariant extension to the complete finite-cell configuration space. The threshold tch
below is chosen so that the entire curvature-label support lies where this cutoff equals one.
Lemma 3.1 (Representation-normalized charge and derivative ledger). For every fixed G, faithful ρ,
and integer 0 ≤ r ≤ 2sG + 3, there are finite constants depending only on the fixed cell atlas such that

                                wG,ρ (U ) ≥ 0,      wG,ρ (U ) = 0 ⇐⇒ U = e,

                             cG ∥X∥2 ≤ wG,ρ (eX ) ≤ CG ∥X∥2       (∥X∥ ≤ r0,G ),
and
             ∥DwG,ρ (U )∥ ≤ CG wG,ρ (U )1/2 ,       ∥Dr wG,ρ (U )∥ ≤ CG,r     (2 ≤ r ≤ 2sG + 3).
The same bounds, with finite template constants, hold after composition with every Wilson word, retained
plaquette word, reflected word, or seam word used below.
Proof. Nonnegativity and the zero set are Proposition 1.2. In the chosen invariant metric the Hessian of
wG,ρ at the identity is positive definite; Taylor’s theorem gives the two-sided quadratic comparison. The
first-derivative estimate follows from that comparison on the identity tube. On its compact complement
wG,ρ has a positive minimum while its derivative is bounded, so the same inequality holds globally after
enlarging the constant. All higher derivatives are bounded because wG,ρ is a smooth matrix coefficient
on compact G. Every word in the fixed path atlas is a finite composition of multiplication and inversion,
so the chain rule gives the asserted finite derivative bounds.

Lemma 3.2 (Simultaneous selected-anchor derivative). Select the first fine link of every straight two-link
anchor as the anchor variable; these links are distinct. Hold every remaining fine link in a detail list Z,
and write the simultaneous retained-coordinate map in product exponential charts as
                                                 b Z : a 7−→ w.

                                    b Z (a) = ϑ(I + KZ (a)),            7

                                                                                  1

                               a                                                  4
The same row and column estimates hold after the fixed marked derivatives used below.
Proof. At the flat field, the selected straight input contributes ϑI. The cross-anchor cancellation can be
read directly from the flat forty-nine-path symbol. Its diagonal detour part is proportional to
                                                      kµ Y
                                              cos          cos kλ ,
                                                       2
                                                        λ̸=µ

so in position space every detour coefficient has an odd displacement in all three transverse directions
relative to the selected all-even anchor column. For µ ̸= ν, the factor
                                        kν
                           sin kµ sin      (2 cos kα cos kβ + cos kα + cos kβ + 2)
                                        2
has an odd µ displacement. Thus no detour term lands on another selected anchor link; the orientation
signs are the sine signs in this formula. The flat simultaneous derivative is therefore exactly ϑI. Away
from the flat field, every entry is an analytic function of finitely many path holonomies and differs from
its flat value by O(g(1 + Rg )). The forty-nine-path tubes have fixed row and column incidence. Shrinking
the common weak threshold makes the two Schur sums at most 1/4. Chart-trivialization and every fixed

multiplication maps are entire, while inversion and the principal logarithm are holomorphic on a fixed
neighborhood of the identity. At the flat field the Karcher critical point has positive Hessian on the
retained tangent space; the analytic implicit-function theorem therefore supplies a unique holomorphic
Karcher mean on a fixed complex neighborhood. The path family and the reflected template family are
finite, so one may choose common nested radii 0 < η0 < η1 for all of them. On the larger finite-dimensional
complex polydisc, every elementary map and their finite compositions are holomorphic and uniformly
bounded. Cauchy’s integral estimate on the smaller polydisc gives uniform derivative bounds through
order three with denominator powers of η1 − η0 .
   The global block is obtained by translating this fixed finite cell. Each output row meets only a fixed
number of input cells, and each input column enters only a fixed number of output cells. Multiplying the
finite-cell Cauchy bounds by these fixed incidence numbers gives the two-sided exponentially weighted
Schur-kernel bounds. These incidence numbers do not depend on the total volume or preceding weak
depth. The aligned-reflection family is finite, so one takes a finite maximum.

Let TQ be a fixed smooth invariant dispersion function formed from the squared relative-loop distances of
the forty-nine path inputs. It vanishes on the flat set, its small sublevel set lies inside the common Karcher
chart, and it uses only one fixed enlarged-cell template. On that sublevel set the retained holonomies

that is one on a prescribed smaller Karcher sublevel and zero before the edge of the larger Karcher tube;
this gives a smooth invariant extension to the complete finite-cell configuration space. The threshold tch
below is chosen so that the entire curvature-label support lies where this cutoff equals one.
Lemma 3.1 (Representation-normalized charge and derivative ledger). For every fixed G, faithful ρ,
and integer 0 ≤ r ≤ 2sG + 3, there are finite constants depending only on the fixed cell atlas such that

                                wG,ρ (U ) ≥ 0,      wG,ρ (U ) = 0 ⇐⇒ U = e,

                             cG ∥X∥2 ≤ wG,ρ (eX ) ≤ CG ∥X∥2       (∥X∥ ≤ r0,G ),
and
             ∥DwG,ρ (U )∥ ≤ CG wG,ρ (U )1/2 ,       ∥Dr wG,ρ (U )∥ ≤ CG,r     (2 ≤ r ≤ 2sG + 3).
The same bounds, with finite template constants, hold after composition with every Wilson word, retained
plaquette word, reflected word, or seam word used below.
Proof. Nonnegativity and the zero set are Proposition 1.2. In the chosen invariant metric the Hessian of
wG,ρ at the identity is positive definite; Taylor’s theorem gives the two-sided quadratic comparison. The
first-derivative estimate follows from that comparison on the identity tube. On its compact complement
wG,ρ has a positive minimum while its derivative is bounded, so the same inequality holds globally after
enlarging the constant. All higher derivatives are bounded because wG,ρ is a smooth matrix coefficient
on compact G. Every word in the fixed path atlas is a finite composition of multiplication and inversion,
so the chain rule gives the asserted finite derivative bounds.

                                wG,ρ (U ) ≥ 0,      wG,ρ (U ) = 0 ⇐⇒ U = e,

                             cG ∥X∥2 ≤ wG,ρ (eX ) ≤ CG ∥X∥2       (∥X∥ ≤ r0,G ),
and
             ∥DwG,ρ (U )∥ ≤ CG wG,ρ (U )1/2 ,       ∥Dr wG,ρ (U )∥ ≤ CG,r     (2 ≤ r ≤ 2sG + 3).
The same bounds, with finite template constants, hold after composition with every Wilson word, retained
plaquette word, reflected word, or seam word used below.
Proof. Nonnegativity and the zero set are Proposition 1.2. In the chosen invariant metric the Hessian of
wG,ρ at the identity is positive definite; Taylor’s theorem gives the two-sided quadratic comparison. The
first-derivative estimate follows from that comparison on the identity tube. On its compact complement
wG,ρ has a positive minimum while its derivative is bounded, so the same inequality holds globally after
enlarging the constant. All higher derivatives are bounded because wG,ρ is a smooth matrix coefficient
on compact G. Every word in the fixed path atlas is a finite composition of multiplication and inversion,
so the chain rule gives the asserted finite derivative bounds.

Lemma 3.2 (Simultaneous selected-anchor derivative). Select the first fine link of every straight two-link
anchor as the anchor variable; these links are distinct. Hold every remaining fine link in a detail list Z,
and write the simultaneous retained-coordinate map in product exponential charts as
                                                 b Z : a 7−→ w.
                                                 B

On the common weak chart,
                                    b Z (a) = ϑ(I + KZ (a)),            7

                                                                                  1
                              sup max{∥KZ (a)∥1→1 , ∥KZ (a)∥∞→∞ } ≤                 .
                               a                                                  4
The same row and column estimates hold after the fixed marked derivatives used below.
Proof. At the flat field, the selected straight input contributes ϑI. The cross-anchor cancellation can be
read directly from the flat forty-nine-path symbol. Its diagonal detour part is proportional to
                                                      kµ Y
                                              cos          cos kλ ,
                                                       2
                                                        λ̸=µ

so in position space every detour coefficient has an odd displacement in all three transverse directions
relative to the selected all-even anchor column. For µ ̸= ν, the factor
                                        kν
                           sin kµ sin      (2 cos kα cos kβ + cos kα + cos kβ + 2)
                                        2
has an odd µ displacement. Thus no detour term lands on another selected anchor link; the orientation
signs are the sine signs in this formula. The flat simultaneous derivative is therefore exactly ϑI. Away
from the flat field, every entry is an analytic function of finitely many path holonomies and differs from
its flat value by O(g(1 + Rg )). The forty-nine-path tubes have fixed row and column incidence. Shrinking
the common weak threshold makes the two Schur sums at most 1/4. Chart-trivialization and every fixed
mark add only another finite-incidence O(g(1 + Rg )) term.

Theorem 3.3 (Fixed-chart global anchor coordinate). For fixed details Z, the map B
                                                                                 b Z is bi-Lipschitz
on the convex product chart and a diffeomorphism onto its image. More precisely,
                             3                      b Z (b)∥2 ≤ ϑ 5 ∥a − b∥2 .

                             4                                    4
Every principal subproblem obtained by fixing the exterior anchors has the same lower Lipschitz bound
and a volume-independent inverse derivative.
Proof. For v = a − b, integrate the derivative along the segment from b to a. Schur’s test gives
∥KZ ∥2→2 ≤ 1/4, proving both bounds. The lower bound gives injectivity; the inverse-function theorem
gives the smooth inverse. A principal submatrix of KZ has no larger row or column sum, so the same proof
applies with exterior anchors fixed. Higher fixed marked derivatives follow by differentiating (I + KZ )−1
and using its Neumann series.

Theorem 3.9 (Corrected support closure). Assume:
1. the summed replica-BKAR theorem;
2. the geometric reporting lemma;
3. the complete-species allocation cone;
4. the exact dimension-ledger reblocking factor 24−∆ ;
5. a logarithmic-support localization estimate
                           qloc (D, L) ≤ C L−3 + (log L)pD LΓ−δD ,
                                                               
                                                                                      δD → ∞.
Choose κ = µ/2. If the rooted regular atom mass and allocation radius are small enough that
                                                     χeff (u, r, b) < 1,
then one may choose finite D and finite L = 2s so that the stable regular derivative satisfies
                                                 ∥DΦreg |stable ∥ < 1
in the same permanent κ-weighted support norm.
Proof. The first two lemmas leave a residual connector decay µ − κ > 0 and convert every fixed geometric
and regulator cost into a per-vertex constant. The summed BKAR tree recursion is therefore geometric
in the effective rooted link mass χeff . Reblocking is paid by the engineering factor and does not weaken κ.
Choose D with δD > Γ + 3, then choose finite L so qloc lies inside the strict associated-graded jet gap.
Finally shrink u, r, b to absorb the nonlinear and irregular source terms.

3.3    Exterior complex tube
Fix the exact symmetric forty-nine-path fixed-G block. Let X be a connected curvature-bad component,
I(X) its fixed integration core, and RX its exterior ring. Use the selected-anchor component chart on
I(X), with every chart Jacobian included in the density.
Lemma 3.12 (Finite word transport). There are fixed radii δout , δin > 0 and a constant Ctr < ∞,
uniform under the standing convention, such that the component anchor solve is holomorphic for

                                       η ∈ WRX ,        h ∈ Θδout ,RX ,

and every Wilson word in the owned core atlas obeys
                               X
                                    ∥δAℓ (h)∥2 ≤ Ctr u qRX (h) + |X| .
                                                                    

                                ℓ∈I(X)

Its derivatives through order three have the same rooted local incidence bound.
Proof. Every owned plaquette is a finite word in the fixed forty-nine-path atlas. On the smaller tube, the
exponential, logarithm, Karcher solve, and principal-submatrix anchor inverse are holomorphic. Their
derivatives are bounded on the compact closure of each local template tube. There are only finitely many
bulk and aligned-reflection local templates; periodic wrapping does not change a rooted local template.
Summing the finite local row/column incidence bounds gives the displayed estimate.

                                       p


Away from that tube, w has a positive compact minimum, while its first two derivatives are bounded;
enlarging CG gives the same inequalities on the complete fixed complex tube. The inequality 2ab ≤ a2 + b2 ,
followed by the finite word-transport estimate, gives the component bound. Proposition 3.6 gives the
second display.

Proof. Use the model-specific normal coordinates of Proposition 2.9 on every fixed component cell. Start

and action bounds.

fixed. It therefore gives a unique component anchor correction with constants independent of shape and
topology. Fresh details√are the remaining fiber coordinates. The finite incidence and inverse bounds show
that a ball of radius c u remains inside the common coordinate tube and on the same exact fiber.

derivatives are bounded on the compact closure of each local template tube. There are only finitely many
bulk and aligned-reflection local templates; periodic wrapping does not change a rooted local template.
Summing the finite local row/column incidence bounds gives the displayed estimate.
Lemma 3.13 (Complex Wilson-sector reserve). For h in the fixed exterior tube,

                                                 1
                           Re AX (z; η + h) ≥      AX (z; η) − Cu qRX (h) + |X| .
                                                                               
                                                 2

By Proposition 3.6, on the curvature-bad support,
                                                                 |X|
                                             AX (z; η) ≥ c∗ χ2       ,
                                                                 νov
and therefore
                                                     c∗ χ2 |X|
                          u−1 Re AX (z; η + h) ≥               − CqRX (h) − C|X|.
                                                     2 u νov
Proof. For one normalized fixed-G Wilson word, put w(U ) = wG,ρ (U ). The zero of w is nondegenerate
at the identity. On a small identity tube, Taylor expansion in a tangent increment H gives

                     |∂w(U )[H]| ≤ CG w(U ) ∥H∥,        |∂ 2 w(U )[H, H]| ≤ CG ∥H∥2 .
                                       p


Away from that tube, w has a positive compact minimum, while its first two derivatives are bounded;
enlarging CG gives the same inequalities on the complete fixed complex tube. The inequality 2ab ≤ a2 + b2 ,
followed by the finite word-transport estimate, gives the component bound. Proposition 3.6 gives the
second display.
Lemma 3.14 (Fixed-atlas residual). After removing the scalar, complete retained two-jet, and allo-
cated local determinant endpoint, the sum of Wilson, Haar, inverse-coordinate, coarea, anchor-return,
determinant-remainder, and finite Schur-connector terms satisfies

                         Re Lfixed

on a smaller fixed tube, for some ωfix > 0.

third-order integral remainder is bounded by

                               C∥z∥3phys ,      ∥z∥phys ≤ u1/2−ϵ ,       ϵ < 1/6.

The corresponding complex Hessian is O(u1/2−ϵ ). More explicitly, after their true scalar and quadratic

using the uniform optical inverse; convolution of its exponential kernels preserves a positive exponent m.
Rooted local incidence gives the result with
                                                          1
                                                 ωfix =     − ϵ > 0,
                                                          2
the smaller exponent being the complex-Hessian cost used by the exterior regulator rather than the
zero-row cubic remainder.
Lemma 3.15 (Inherited sectorial lifting). Let the incoming regular interaction be holomorphic on the
permanent tube, real on the real slice, and satisfy

                                                  [R]∂,C;a;3 ≤ r.

Then the inherited terms owned by X obey

                           Re Rinh
                               X (η + h) ≤ Cr|X| + CrqRX (η) + CrqRX (h).

Proof. Taylor expansion first on the real segment and then on the imaginary segment gives
                                                                            
       Re RY (η + ih) ≤ RY (0) + C∥DRY (0)∥2 + C 1 + sup ∥D2 RY (η + ith)∥ qY (η) + qY (h) .
                                                                                          
                                                                  t

The linear imaginary term has zero real part because RY is real on the real slice. Rooted support
summation over the activities owned by X gives the result without a raw volume incidence factor.
Theorem 3.16 (Exterior tube and analytic residual). Assume the incoming regular interaction lies in
the reproduced regular complex domain and

                                             C (uωfix + r) < b1 .

Then the curvature-bad density is holomorphic on one fixed exterior/source tube and its complete post-

                            Re Lan

Proof. Holomorphy follows from the fixed-domain component chart, the finite word transport lemma, and
the reproduced regular complex tube. Add the fixed-atlas and inherited residual bounds. The smallness
condition absorbs their quadratic coefficient into b1 . Combining with the Wilson-sector reserve and taking
absolute values before integration gives the final estimate.

3.4    Component plateau and denominator
Let X be a bad seed with integration core I(X), reported hull H(X), and exterior ring RX = H(X)\I(X).
Let W be its retained field and η the regular ring datum. All X-owned Haar, coordinate, coarea,
determinant, and inherited factors are included in the reference density ρref
                                                                          X (z; η), and
                                               Z
                                      GX (η) = ρrefX (z; η) dµX (z).


Lemma 3.17 (Exact component section). There are ctr , Ctr > 0, independent of X, scale, volume, weak
depth, wrapping, and reflected template, such that the exact fiber over (W, η) contains an analytic section
sX (W, η) satisfying
                           ∥sX (W, η)∥∞ ≤ Ctr Rg ,      AI(X) (sX ) ≤ Ctr uRg2 |X|.
                                                            √
A product ball in the fresh detail coordinates of radius ctr u about sX remains in the same exact fiber.


                                                          16
Proof. Use the model-specific normal coordinates of Proposition 2.9 on every fixed component cell. Start
from the canonical weak prolongation of W : split each coarse logarithm equally between the two straight
fine links, set transverse details to zero, and interpolate across the fixed-width boundary templates to the
actual weak ring datum η. This gives a globally consistent configuration with the displayed pointwise
and action bounds.
   The component-owned anchor problem is a principal subproblem of the simultaneous selected-anchor
map. Theorem 3.3 supplies a uniform lower Lipschitz bound and inverse derivative with the ring anchors
fixed. It therefore gives a unique component anchor correction with constants independent of shape and
topology. Fresh details√are the remaining fiber coordinates. The finite incidence and inverse bounds show
that a ball of radius c u remains inside the common coordinate tube and on the same exact fiber.
Lemma 3.18 (Good-cutoff plateau). If

                                        χ2
                                           ≥ K Rg2 + 1 + log u−1
                                                                 
                                        u
with K sufficiently large, then, after shrinking the common weak threshold, every owned good cutoff is
identically one throughout the ball of Lemma 3.17.
Proof. Finite-word Taylor bounds on the section and the detail ball give, on each owned cell,

                                         DQ + TQ ≤ CuRg2 + Cu.

The displayed dominance condition makes this less than both χ2 /8 and tch /8 after one common threshold
reduction. By the cutoff definitions in Section 3, χin
                                                    Q = 1 on the complete ball.

Lemma 3.19 (Reference trial plateau). There are fixed d0 < ∞, c, C > 0 such that
                                                   h             2
                                                                         i|X|
                               eb0 qRX (η) GX (η) ≥ cud0 /2 e−C(Rg +1+r)      .

All constants are uniform under the standing convention and in X.
Proof. Integrate only over the plateau ball. Its number of real coordinates is at most d0 |X|, hence its
product volume is bounded below by (cud0 /2 )|X| . On that ball the Wilson action divided by u costs at
most CRg2 |X|. Every fixed Haar, coordinate, coarea, determinant, and anchor-return factor is bounded
above and below on the common finite atlas. The reproduced regular interaction and the cutoff-free
boundary reserve of the exterior-tube theorem cost at most

                                         C(1 + r)|X| + b0 qRX (η).

All good cutoffs equal one by Lemma 3.18. Multiplying the bounds and moving the exterior regulator to
the left gives the result.

topology. Fresh details√are the remaining fiber coordinates. The finite incidence and inverse bounds show
that a ball of radius c u remains inside the common coordinate tube and on the same exact fiber.
Lemma 3.18 (Good-cutoff plateau). If

                                        χ2
                                           ≥ K Rg2 + 1 + log u−1
                                                                 
                                        u
with K sufficiently large, then, after shrinking the common weak threshold, every owned good cutoff is
identically one throughout the ball of Lemma 3.17.
Proof. Finite-word Taylor bounds on the section and the detail ball give, on each owned cell,

                                         DQ + TQ ≤ CuRg2 + Cu.

The displayed dominance condition makes this less than both χ2 /8 and tch /8 after one common threshold
reduction. By the cutoff definitions in Section 3, χin
                                                    Q = 1 on the complete ball.

Lemma 3.19 (Reference trial plateau). There are fixed d0 < ∞, c, C > 0 such that
                                                   h             2
                                                                         i|X|
                               eb0 qRX (η) GX (η) ≥ cud0 /2 e−C(Rg +1+r)      .

All constants are uniform under the standing convention and in X.
Proof. Integrate only over the plateau ball. Its number of real coordinates is at most d0 |X|, hence its
product volume is bounded below by (cud0 /2 )|X| . On that ball the Wilson action divided by u costs at
most CRg2 |X|. Every fixed Haar, coordinate, coarea, determinant, and anchor-return factor is bounded
above and below on the common finite atlas. The reproduced regular interaction and the cutoff-free
boundary reserve of the exterior-tube theorem cost at most

                                         C(1 + r)|X| + b0 qRX (η).

All good cutoffs equal one by Lemma 3.18. Multiplying the bounds and moving the exterior regulator to
the left gives the result.

                                                     18
3.6    Irregular ownership and C 1 flatness
The irregular map must be assembled from the exact labelled density before any absolute-value estimate
is taken. This section fixes the ownership rule that was previously left as four composition hypotheses.
   Let X = {X1 , . . . , Xm } be a compatible family of bad cores. Compatibility means that the enlarged
integration cores I + (Xi ) are pairwise disjoint. Every pre-logarithmic local factor carries its actual finite
support; every exponentially local inverse, coarea, or Schur factor is first expanded in the permanent
exponential matrix algebra and each term is reported on its canonical connected hull.
Definition 3.22 (Canonical owner). For a reported connected factor A with support S(A) define
                               
                               Xi ,
                                        S(A) ∩ I(Xi ) ̸= ∅ and S(A) ∩ I(Xj ) = ∅ (j ̸= i),
                ownX (A) = cross, S(A) meets at least two integration cores,
                                 reg,    S(A) meets no integration core.
                               
                               

If a fixed-template boundary factor meets an integration core only through its prescribed ring, it is
assigned to that core precisely when its canonical root lies in I(Xi ); otherwise it remains regular. Ties
are therefore impossible.
Definition 3.23 (Gaussian/determinant allocation separation). For the exact joint fresh-detail law, let
H0 denote the finite-range ambient precision before the Karcher constraint and let Qopt be the actual
constrained precision of Theorem 4.9. Fix a separation collar RG . Cores closer than RG after their
ordinary interaction collars are merged into one core. A remaining compatible family satisfies:
(DAS1) every determinant endpoint whose two-jet enters the reference has an actual support of diameter
         at most the fixed number Rdet ;
(DAS2) complementary projectors Pvac + Pret + Popt + Pirr = I allocate every endpoint two-jet exactly
         once;
(DAS3) all stagewise Gaussian conditionals are the chain-rule disintegration of one normalized joint law,
         with scalar normalization retained once at the exterior endpoint;
(DAS4) H0 is finite range, while Qopt and its inverse have uniform exponentially decaying kernels;
(DAS5) for distinct compatible cores,
                    X                  ′
               sup       eaG d(I(X),I(X )) ∥Qopt,I(X),I(X ′ ) ∥[3] ≤ κG (RG ), κG (RG ) ≤ CG e−mG RG . (1)
                X
                    X ′ ̸=X

Lemma 3.24 (The exact forty-nine-path block satisfies DAS). The Wilson Hessian, local Haar and
coarea endpoint two-jets, the optical projector, and the compatible-core geometry satisfy Definition 3.23,
uniformly in finite volume and weak depth. The collar RG may be chosen once so that κG (RG ) < 1/8.
Proof. The Wilson plaquette Hessian and Feynman grounding term have fixed finite range. The forty-nine-
path constraint and each chart/coarea endpoint live on one fixed block template. The coefficient projectors
are the complementary projections of the complete two-jet extraction, and the stagewise conditionals are
chain-rule factors of one normalized joint law. The actual constrained precision is Qopt = T ∗ Hf T . The
analytic-strip proof of Corollary 4.10 applies to T , Hf , and Qopt itself, so both precision and covariance
have uniform exponential kernel rows. Restricting that row to two cores separated by RG gives (1).
Merging the finitely many cores that violate the separation condition preserves the action charge and
leaves one compatible family. Enlarge RG until the displayed smallness holds.

For every compatible X the owner map of Definition 3.22 gives the exact identity
                                 X                X
                                   dA = Dreg +        DX + Dcross ,
                                      A               X∈X

where each connected term occurs once. The same statement holds for the Haar-coordinate logarithm,
normal-section return factors, local determinant remainders, exponentially reported Schur factors, and
the connected Gaussian normalization below.


                                                      19
Proof. In the exponential matrix algebra, ∥DN P ∥ < η < 1 and the matrix logarithm has its absolutely
convergent word expansion. Report every word on the union of its canonical connector paths. The
permanent support estimate pays all convolutions. Every reported word has one connected support, so
the owner cases are disjoint and exhaustive. The other factors are treated by their Taylor or matrix-log
expansions in the same algebra.

3.6    Irregular ownership and C 1 flatness
The irregular map must be assembled from the exact labelled density before any absolute-value estimate
is taken. This section fixes the ownership rule that was previously left as four composition hypotheses.
   Let X = {X1 , . . . , Xm } be a compatible family of bad cores. Compatibility means that the enlarged
integration cores I + (Xi ) are pairwise disjoint. Every pre-logarithmic local factor carries its actual finite
support; every exponentially local inverse, coarea, or Schur factor is first expanded in the permanent
exponential matrix algebra and each term is reported on its canonical connected hull.
Definition 3.22 (Canonical owner). For a reported connected factor A with support S(A) define
                               
                               Xi ,
                                        S(A) ∩ I(Xi ) ̸= ∅ and S(A) ∩ I(Xj ) = ∅ (j ̸= i),
                ownX (A) = cross, S(A) meets at least two integration cores,
                                 reg,    S(A) meets no integration core.
                               
                               

If a fixed-template boundary factor meets an integration core only through its prescribed ring, it is
assigned to that core precisely when its canonical root lies in I(Xi ); otherwise it remains regular. Ties
are therefore impossible.
Definition 3.23 (Gaussian/determinant allocation separation). For the exact joint fresh-detail law, let
H0 denote the finite-range ambient precision before the Karcher constraint and let Qopt be the actual
constrained precision of Theorem 4.9. Fix a separation collar RG . Cores closer than RG after their
ordinary interaction collars are merged into one core. A remaining compatible family satisfies:
(DAS1) every determinant endpoint whose two-jet enters the reference has an actual support of diameter
         at most the fixed number Rdet ;
(DAS2) complementary projectors Pvac + Pret + Popt + Pirr = I allocate every endpoint two-jet exactly
         once;
(DAS3) all stagewise Gaussian conditionals are the chain-rule disintegration of one normalized joint law,
         with scalar normalization retained once at the exterior endpoint;
(DAS4) H0 is finite range, while Qopt and its inverse have uniform exponentially decaying kernels;
(DAS5) for distinct compatible cores,
                    X                  ′
               sup       eaG d(I(X),I(X )) ∥Qopt,I(X),I(X ′ ) ∥[3] ≤ κG (RG ), κG (RG ) ≤ CG e−mG RG . (1)
                X
                    X ′ ̸=X

Lemma 3.24 (The exact forty-nine-path block satisfies DAS). The Wilson Hessian, local Haar and
coarea endpoint two-jets, the optical projector, and the compatible-core geometry satisfy Definition 3.23,
uniformly in finite volume and weak depth. The collar RG may be chosen once so that κG (RG ) < 1/8.
Proof. The Wilson plaquette Hessian and Feynman grounding term have fixed finite range. The forty-nine-
path constraint and each chart/coarea endpoint live on one fixed block template. The coefficient projectors
are the complementary projections of the complete two-jet extraction, and the stagewise conditionals are
chain-rule factors of one normalized joint law. The actual constrained precision is Qopt = T ∗ Hf T . The
analytic-strip proof of Corollary 4.10 applies to T , Hf , and Qopt itself, so both precision and covariance
have uniform exponential kernel rows. Restricting that row to two cores separated by RG gives (1).
Merging the finitely many cores that violate the separation condition preserves the action charge and
leaves one compatible family. Enlarge RG until the displayed smallness holds.

Lemma 3.29 (History compatibility after exact output formation). At every RG step, form the complete
connected output first, assign its canonical reported hull, and only then compute its regulator allocation
and history certificate. Under this rule:
   (i) every input history has one output owner;
  (ii) no input allocation is copied to two incompatible output histories;
 (iii) concatenation of successive certificates is associative;
 (iv) summing over all certificates recovers the exact unlabelled connected output.
Proof. For one step, canonical collect is a partition of the finite set of labelled connected histories by the
least rooted hull in the fixed ordering. Thus there is one owner and the unlabelled sum is unchanged.
The output allocation is a function of the already collected output gradient and complex Hessian, not of
an input branch. Induct on the number of steps. At the next step the complete preceding certificate
is treated as one input label, so a new canonical hull is appended once. This proves associativity and
excludes copying. Forgetting the labels at every stage gives the exact iterated conditional logarithm by
Fubini and the connected exponential formula.

Theorem 4.4 (Summed replica-BKAR admission). Assume the exact simultaneous hard-core/Gaussian
BKAR identity and species rows
                                     ≤N
                                ∥Dq Dmark Fα ∥βα ≤ Aα,N q!σ −q ,           q ≥ 0.

Assume also that the complete species allocation gives

                                                         X
                                                4∥C∥           Bα < 1
                                                         α∈I

for every auxiliary replica family, and that the parent-normalized rooted tree link constant, after the fixed
exponential losses and the factor σ −2 , is strictly below one.
   Then the normalized connected logarithm, summed with its exact 1/n!, converges absolutely in a
weakened rooted support norm and is Fréchet-C N in the declared structural marks, uniformly in volume,
replica number, forest parameters, and previous RG depth.
Proof. Differentiate the BKAR functional along a tree. A hard-core derivative costs one overlap bond
and no field derivative. A covariance derivative costs one covariance connector and one field derivative at
each endpoint. If di covariance edges meet vertex i, the analytic row contributes di !σ −di Aαi ,N .
   The Gram lemma bounds every auxiliary Gaussian regulator moment uniformly in the replica family.
Summing the choices of hard-core versus covariance derivatives costs only 2n−1 . The Pruefer lemma
shows that the exact 1/n! in the connected logarithm absorbs the product of vertex factorials with only
an exponential loss. The remaining rooted tree sum is the ordinary parent-normalized geometric recursion
in the effective link constant. Fixed structural marks are distributed by Leibniz and change only the
exponential constant depending on N .

                                                                         0

Since RP is real on the real slice, DRP (η)[θ] is real, so the linear imaginary term has zero real part. The
displayed estimate follows.

                                          e                                  2
                                               P ∋e

Thus reproduction of the complex boundary seminorm automatically reproduces a regulator-allocation
cone.

Theorem 4.6 (Arbitrary fixed passive-source order). For every prescribed finite N , the integrated
ultraviolet map extends to one rooted domain D(a; 3, N ) with constants uniform in volume, microscopic
cutoff, and preceding weak depth. It is C 3 in the active structural variables and analytic through source

∥ · ∥a;3,N rows.
    In particular, for the irregular map,

                           max ∥DJI Eirr ∥a;3,0 + ∥DR DJI Eirr ∥ ≤ CN u−pN e−cN /u .

                         |I|≤N

Proof. On the regular chart, bounded local source parameters enter holomorphically. Source differentiation
inserts bounded finite-range atoms and does not increase the Gaussian field-derivative degree at a BKAR
vertex. The factorial source norm pays Leibniz and Faà di Bruno combinatorics. The active graph
transform still uses only the structural C 3 rows.

                             e−bq(η) GX (η)−1 ∥Dstr

For transition and outer labels, differentiate the smooth finite-dimensional density directly. Every cutoff
derivative remains supported on the same charged event. For fixed N , the resulting Bell-polynomial loss
is a fixed polynomial in |X|, u−1 , and the chart constants, and is absorbed by the bad-action exponential.
   After compatible-core composition, a rooted decorated tree with V vertices has at most V N placements
of N labelled source leaves. For 0 < m < 1,

Combining this with the fixed susceptibility reserve for active structural marks and one R-derivative
proves the displayed irregular bound.

4.3    Gaussian reference and optical covariance
This section constructs the Gaussian objects used by the weak block, Wick coordinates, replica forests,
and source connectors. Two objects must not be conflated:
1. the fixed tangent Feynman Gaussian on mean-zero lattice one-forms;
2. its exact conditional detail Gaussian for the linearized forty-nine-path Karcher block.
The first carries the massless retained field. The second is an optical, high-pass covariance with a uniform
gap. The nonlinear Wilson action, Haar and exponential-coordinate density, Karcher coarea factor, cutoffs,
inherited interaction, and source factors are not hidden in either covariance; they are exposed as exact
activities after the normal-fiber change of variables.
4.3.1 Grounded Feynman field and retained torons
Let Λ be a periodic four-dimensional lattice. Write AΛ = ℓ2 (E(Λ); g) for real Lie-algebra-valued one-forms
with the normalized edge scalar product. Let d0 and d1 be the lattice coboundaries from zero-forms to
one-forms and from one-forms to plaquette two-forms. Put
                                               n                   o
                                        A0Λ = A ∈ AΛ : A(0)
                                                          b     =0 .

Constant connection modes are retained compact toron/background variables; they are never inverted or
integrated by the Gaussian reference.
Lemma 4.7 (Exact lattice Feynman scalarization). On A0Λ ,

                                     QF := d∗1 d1 + d0 d∗0 = ∆Λ ⊗ I4 ⊗ Ig .

At momentum p ̸= 0 its symbol is

                                                                                                   F and
the transverse covariance have the same matrix elements between J and J ′ .

Proof. At nonzero momentum let δµ (p) = eipµ − 1. Then
                                   d∗1 d1 = ωI4 − δδ ∗ ,     d0 d∗0 = δδ ∗ ,
which proves the first two statements. Since d∗0 d∗1 = 0, every curl current is transverse. The difference
between the Feynman and transverse covariances is longitudinal and therefore vanishes between such
currents. The finite color factor is diagonal. The omitted zero momentum is precisely the retained toron
sector.

derivatives costs only 2n−1 . The Pruefer lemma
shows that the exact 1/n! in the connected logarithm absorbs the product of vertex factorials with only
an exponential loss. The remaining rooted tree sum is the ordinary parent-normalized geometric recursion
in the effective link constant. Fixed structural marks are distributed by Leibniz and change only the
exponential constant depending on N .
