4.3.3 Exact Gaussian disintegration and harmonic extension
Let C≥j be the mean-zero Feynman covariance before the scale-j block and let Bj = 12 Af,j be the
normalized linearized retained map. On the mean-zero coarse space define

                                 Cjc = Bj C≥j Bj∗ ,        Jj = C≥j Bj∗ (Cjc )−1 ,

and
                                              Cjd = C≥j − Jj Cjc Jj∗ .
All inverses are taken only on the stated mean-zero spaces; torons remain in the retained compact
background.
Theorem 4.11 (Exact linear Gaussian fiber decomposition). The operators above satisfy

                           Bj Jj = I,        Bj Cjd = 0,       C≥j = Cjd + Jj Cjc Jj∗ .

If W ∼ N (0, Cjc ) and ζ ∼ N (0, Cjd ) are independent, then

                                                  A = Jj W + ζ

has covariance C≥j and Bj A = W . Conversely, this is the exact conditional disintegration of the fine
Feynman Gaussian given the retained linear block. In the folded Fourier frame, Cjd is the pushforward of
Copt from Corollary 4.10.
Proof. These are the finite-dimensional Gaussian Schur-complement identities. Since Cjc = Bj C≥j Bj∗ ,
Bj Jj = I. Substitution gives Bj Cjd = 0 and the covariance sum. The characteristic function of Jj W + ζ is
therefore the one with covariance C≥j . The folded optical parameterization T (k) identifies the conditional
detail space with ker Af (k), and the conditional precision is the restriction of Hf to that space.


                                                        28
  After canonical scale-two rescaling, define C≥j+1 to be the rescaled coarse covariance Cjc . Thus the
notation

used below means the exact identity of Theorem 4.11, with Cj = Cjd and with the remaining covariance
embedded by Jj before the canonical identification. It is not a declaration of an unconstructed translation-
invariant product slicing.

field,
                           Eζ∼Cjd : P (Jj W + ζ) :C≥j =: (P ◦ Jj )(W ) :Cjc .

The six one-step maps.          The exact regular step is organized in the following order.
(E1) Normal chart return. Substitute the finite-volume analytic normal section into the local Wilson,
     Haar, inherited, and source factors.


                                                              34
(E2) Coarea logarithm. Append the field-dependent logarithm

                                                  − log det(I + DN P ),

     with the fixed linear determinant owned once outside the activity map.
(E3) Summed connected logarithm. Apply the simultaneous hard-core/Gaussian replica-BKAR
     identity and sum the exact 1/n! series.
(E4) Local projection. Extract the complete gauge-covariant Wick-local jet and retain the short Taylor
     remainder.
(E5) Canonical hull and collect. Report covariance paths and finite collars, assign each history to
     one canonical hull, and collect exactly once.
(E6) Output recertification. Derive the next regulator allocation from the actual post-localization
     output gradient and complex Hessian.

Permanent support exponent.
Lemma 4.24 (One common support exponent). Suppose the covariance connector has decay exponent
µ > 0, and the normal chart, inverse trees, coarea logarithm, and fixed local atlas have positive exponential
locality exponents
                                      mchart , minv , mcoa , matlas > 0.
Choose
                                0 < κ < min{µ, mchart , minv , mcoa , matlas }.
Then all six maps act in the same κ-weighted rooted support space. No per-step weakening of κ is required.

Proof. For a BKAR tree, report every covariance edge by a shortest path and every hard-core edge by its
fixed collar. The geometric reporting inequality gives
                                        X           X
                               τ (P ) ≤   τ (Si ) +    de + Cgeo (n − 1).
                                           i            e∈Tcov


Multiplication by eκτ (P ) leaves the residual connector decay e−(µ−κ)de . Every fixed chart, inverse, coarea,
and atlas branch is summable at exponent κ. Localization costs a polynomial in the rooted radius but
does not alter the exponent. Canonical collect is a partition of histories, so it does not duplicate a support
weight.

Lemma 4.25 (One common analytic species tube). There is a radius ηcom > 0, independent of volume
and preceding weak depth, on which the Wilson word, exponential-coordinate Haar density, Karcher
normal return, coarea logarithm, and finite Schur endpoint are simultaneously holomorphic. On the closed
half-radius tube, their derivatives through structural order three and every fixed passive-source order have
finite fixed-template suprema.

Proof. Each species is a finite composition of multiplication, inversion, exponential, logarithm, the
finite-cell Karcher mean, and an inverse of one of the matrices

                                         I + DN P,          Hopt + B(A).


                                                        35
The path-atlas theorem supplies a positive holomorphy radius for the first five maps. The uniform
inequalities ∥DN P ∥ < 1 and Hopt ≥ 2I persist on a smaller positive tube, so the two inverses are
holomorphic there by a Neumann series. There are only finitely many bulk and aligned-reflection
templates. Taking the minimum of their radii gives ηcom > 0; Cauchy’s estimate on the closed half-radius
tube gives the uniform derivative constants.
   For each fixed local template define the finite constants CW , CHaar , Ccoa , Cchart , CSch as the suprema
on that closed common tube of the third derivatives of respectively the Wilson word, Haar logarithm,
coarea logarithm, normal return, and finite Schur endpoint after their scalar and complete two-jet parts
have been removed. Let Cinc be the largest rooted incidence number of those templates and let Cconn be
the row sum of the exponential covariance connector of Corollary 4.14. All these constants are finite and
uniform under the standing convention.

Proof. Proposition 4.26 bounds the complete species mass by δBKAR . The definition of Calloc gives the

pays the labelled-tree degree factorials. After the fixed support and Cauchy costs, every parent-normalized

Lemma 4.28 (Finite uniform module constants). On the common weak tube,
                             chart

with constants uniform under the standing convention.
Proof. The finite-volume normal fiber equation has a uniform contraction parameter η < 1; its derivatives
are finite products of the stable resolvent, P , D2 N , and D3 N in the exponential matrix algebra. The
coarea logarithm is the finite-volume analytic matrix logarithm of I + DN P , whose inverse is bounded
by (1 − η)−1 .
   The localization projector has a finite template norm polynomial in the support radius. The reported
shortest connector paths and finite collars preserve exponent κ. Canonical hull ownership assigns each
history once.

Six-factor composition.
Theorem 4.29 (Permanent one-step envelope). For every prescribed finite source order N , there are
finite constants
                                 Km < ∞,       0 ≤ m ≤ 3,
independent of volume, microscopic cutoff, and preceding weak depth, such that the exact regular one-step
map satisfies
                                         ∥Dm Φreg ∥κ;3,N ≤ Km
on one reproduced weak ball.
   More explicitly,
                                 chart    coa    BKAR    loc    hull    rec
                                                                            
                        Km ≤ Bm K≤m    , K≤m  , K≤m   , K≤m  , K≤m   , K≤m    ,
where Bm is the finite multivariable Bell polynomial supplied by the chain rule through order m.
Proof. Compose the six bounded maps. Through order three, the chain rule contains only finitely
many partitions. Every term is bounded in the same permanent κ-weighted norm and carries the same
active/source inventory.
   The input allocation cone is an assumption of the weak activity ball; the output cone is derived after
the exact connected output by recertification. Choose the ball so
                                                  C(uω + r) ≤ b∗
and the derived output allocation is at most b∗ . This is an invariant ball inequality, not circular use of
the output theorem as an input.
